{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_vrtx_011Ch5SPczJrF6QRFJU2pYD7","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've changed the code that launches the ship manually from a rotating orbital station. It builds cleanly, but I couldn't confirm it fixes what's in your recording: `/app/.harbor_assets/recording.mp4` isn't in the image (the folder is empty), and I couldn't run the game here. I found the likely cause by reading the docking and launch code.\n\n**What I think the recording shows:** the player takes off from a pad inside a rotating orbital station with the autopilot off. That path (`Player::DoFixspeedTakeoff`, called from `SpaceStation::LaunchShip`) is meant to lift the ship clear of the pad, but it doesn't:\n\n- **Too little clearance.** After a manual landing, the ship is parked with its landing point exactly on the pad's plane. Two things can leave it overlapping the pad:\n  - The ship's collision shape can reach below its landing point.\n  - The station's pad surface can sit a little above that plane.\n\n  The launch code lifted the ship only 0.01 m, so the two collision shapes were still touching when it started.\n- **Too slow to separate.** The ship pulled away at only 0.05 m/s. Because the station spins, a ship that isn't actively thrusting drifts back toward pads that face its axis.\n- **Any contact counts as a crash.** By this point the station has already released the ship's bay, so any touch is treated as hitting the station and damages the hull. That's why the damage starts before the ship visibly hits anything.\n\n**The fix** (`src/Player.cpp`, `Player::DoFixspeedTakeoff`, only the rotating-orbital part):\n- Before launch, the ship is lifted by however far its collision shape reaches below its landing point (taken from the ship model), plus a 1 m margin to cover the pad sitting slightly high.\n- The ship now starts moving away from the pad at the takeoff speed (2 m/s) instead of 0.05 m/s, so there's no slow start.\n- The spin-matching speed is now worked out from the ship's position relative to the station's centre, after the lift. This matches how the cruise control already does it. It gives the same result today because the station sits at the centre of its own frame.\n\nThe fix works the same way for every pad on the station. Nothing else changed: no ship or station data, and no collision or damage rules.\n\n**Not covered:** taking off manually from a ground station still uses the old code, which doesn't lift the ship at all. That didn't seem to match the issue, so I left it alone.
